1. Executive Summary
The applicant, Otsuka Pharmaceutical, submitted the report for Study 405-201-00019, a single-dose,
randomized, double-blind, placebo- and active-controlled crossover study. The study evaluated the
abuse potential of single oral doses of centanafadine API in capsule form (IR formulation) at doses of
200 mg, 400 mg, and 800 mg, compared with placebo and d-amphetamine (15 mg and 30 mg; active
control) in healthy adult recreational drug users with stimulant experience.
Study Population
A total of 112 subjects were enrolled and treated in the Qualification Phase (Part 1). Of these, 62
subjects (55.4%) qualified for the Treatment Phase (Part 2), and 61 subjects (54.5%) were randomized
to the Treatment Phase. Fifty-seven subjects completed the study. Of the completers, 12 subjects were
excluded due to dosing error and 3 subjects met exclusion criteria for the Modified Completer
Population (MCP). Thus, 42 completers were included in the MCP.
Primary Endpoint Results
The primary analysis of Drug Liking VAS Emax demonstrated that centanafadine was significantly
less liked by subjects compared to d-amphetamine with the exception of the comparison between damphetamine 15 mg and centanafadine 800 mg. Due to the closed testing procedure, comparisons
between centanafadine and placebo can only be considered exploratory. The results indicate that the
abuse potential of centanafadine was not similar to placebo.
The individual time course response profiles show that all subjects except two (Subject IDs
and
(b) (6)
) disliked centanafadine 800 mg. The disliking occurred in 83.3% of subjects at 0.25 or 0.5 hours
post-dose and persisted for a prolonged period. Note that when Emax is used as an endpoint, as long
as a subject had a neutral response at any time point, the Emax score would not fall below 50. Similar
but less severe disliking was also observed for the 200 mg and 400 mg centanafadine doses. In such
cases, the key secondary endpoints—Emax scores of Overall Drug Liking VAS and Take Drug Again
VAS—should play important roles in the assessment of the test drug's abuse potential.
(b) (6)

Secondary Endpoint Results
The secondary analysis of Emax of Overall Drug Liking VAS demonstrated that compared to damphetamine, subjects overall significantly less liked centanafadine, and the overall liking produced
by 200 mg centanafadine could not be differentiated from placebo. Mean and median differences
between 400 mg and 800 mg centanafadine doses and placebo were negative. The results from the
analysis of Take Drug Again VAS Emax demonstrated that subjects were significantly less willing to
take centanafadine again compared to d-amphetamine but significantly more willing to take
centanafadine again compared to placebo.
The descriptive statistics for the other 7 VAS Emax secondary endpoints (Any Drug Effects, Bad
Drug Effects, Good Drug Effects, High, Nausea, Agitation/Relaxation, and Alertness/Drowsiness)
showed that compared to d-amphetamine, centanafadine produced similar euphoric effects but greater

4

Reference ID: 5793957

bad effects, nausea and agitation. The similar euphoric effects were observed when comparing
centanafadine 200 mg to d-amphetamine 15 mg, and centanafadine 800 mg to d-amphetamine 30 mg.
For Drug Similarity VAS, on average, subjects rated centanafadine 200 mg as more similar to damphetamine than the 400 mg and 800 mg doses of centanafadine. However, the mean and median
scores produced by centanafadine 200 mg for the drug choice d-amphetamine were 30.3 and 24,
respectively, compared to 51.8 and 57 from d-amphetamine 15 mg, and 71.0 and 74.5 from damphetamine 30 mg.
The assessment using ARCI A and BG scales supported findings from VAS measures—centanafadine
shows less stimulant effect than d-amphetamine.
Conclusion
Study 405-201-00019 demonstrated that centanafadine has less abuse potential than d-amphetamine.

2. Review report on Study 405-201-00019
2.1. Overview
Study 405-201-00019 was a single-dose, randomized, double-blind, placebo- and active-controlled
crossover study to assess the abuse potential of centanafadine (EB-1020) IR compared to d-amphetamine
and placebo in healthy, recreational drug users with stimulant experience.
2.1.1. Objectives of the study
Primary

The primary objective of this study was to evaluate the abuse potential of 3 single oral dose
strengths of centanafadine API in a capsule (IR formulation) compared with placebo and 2 single
oral dose strengths of d-amphetamine in healthy adult recreational drug users with stimulant
experience.
Secondary
No secondary objective(s) was stated.

2.1.2. Study design
This was a single-dose, randomized, double-blind, placebo- and active-controlled crossover study. The
abuse potential of single oral dose administrations of centanafadine API in a capsule (IR formulation)
(200 mg, 400 mg, 800 mg) was compared with that of placebo and d-amphetamine (15 mg, 30 mg; active
control) in healthy recreational drug users with stimulant experience. Subjects participated in a screening
visit (Visit 1) and a single inpatient treatment visit (Visit 2), which included Part 1 Qualification (4 days
inpatient) and Part 2 Treatment (12 days inpatient). The subjects participated in a 7-day (+ 2 days) followup phone call for safety assessment.
Subjects were enrolled in Part 1 Qualification within 23 days of screening. Subjects participated in Part 1
Qualification (inpatient), during which they received oral d-amphetamine 20 mg and placebo in a
randomized, double-blind, crossover manner. The washout period was 24 hours between two treatment
periods. A minimum washout period of 72 hours was used between the last drug administration in Part 1
Qualification and the first drug administration in Part 2 Treatment.
Qualified subjects were randomized to Part 2 Treatment (inpatient). In each treatment period, subjects
received a single oral administration of each of the following 6 treatments in a randomized, double-blind,
crossover manner following an overnight fast:

•
•
•
•
•

Treatment A: centanafadine API in a capsule (IR formulation) 200 mg
Treatment B: centanafadine API in a capsule (IR formulation) 400 mg
Treatment C: centanafadine API in a capsule (IR formulation) 800 mg
Treatment D: d-amphetamine 15 mg
Treatment E: d-amphetamine 30 mg
6

Reference ID: 5793957

•

Treatment F: placebo

The washout period between treatment periods was approximately 48 hours. A follow-up phone call
occurred 7 (+ 2) days after the last treatment period.
The study design schematic is presented in Figure 1.

2.1.3. Analysis populations
The study analysis populations in Part 2 Treatment were defined as follows:
• The Randomized Population will consist of all subjects who are randomized in Part 2 Treatment.
• The Safety Population will consist of all subjects who are randomized and receive at least 1 dose
administration of Investigational medicinal product (IMP) in Part 2 Treatment.
• The Pharmacodynamic Population will consist of all randomized subjects who complete all 6
treatment periods in Part 2 Treatment and who have at least 1 Drug Liking VAS observation
within 2 hours of tmax for each treatment in the trial.
• The Per-protocol Pharmacodynamic Population will consist of all subjects in the
Pharmacodynamic Population who have no major protocol deviations that would impact
pharmacodynamic results.
• The Pharmacokinetic Population will consist of all subjects in the Safety Population who have
sufficient data to calculate at least 1 pharmacokinetic parameter for at least 1 dose of
centanafadine.
Reviewer’s comments: The Pharmacodynamic Population is the Completer Population. The reviewer’s
analyses are based on the Modified Completer Population (MCP) defined as follows:
Modified Completer Population: All subjects in the Completer Population, excluding subjects with the
following elimination criteria: for Drug Liking VAS,

– Emax for d-amphetamine 30 mg ≤55, or
– Emax for placebo – Emax for d-amphetamine 30 mg ≥5, or
– Similar Emax scores (within 5 points difference) across all study treatments
(including placebo).
This population served as the primary population for the pharmacodynamic analyses.
2.1.4. Disposition of subjects
A total of 112 subjects were enrolled and treated in Part 1. Of the 112 subjects treated, 62 subjects
(55.4%) qualified for Part 2 and 61 subjects (54.5%) were randomized in Part 2. One subject that
qualified for Part 2 was not randomized as the subject was discontinued at the end of Part 1 due to a
treatment-emergent adverse event (TEAE) of blood creatine phosphokinase increased.
A total of 51 of 112 subjects (45.5%) were discontinued Part 1. Of the subjects that did not qualify for
Part 2 and were discontinued, most subjects were qualification failures (38 of 112 subjects [33.9%]) that
could not discriminate between placebo and d-amphetamine. Other reasons for discontinuation included
AEs (8 of 112 subjects [7.1%]) and subject withdrawal (5 of 112 subjects [4.5%]).
A total of 61 subjects were treated in Part 2. Fifteen subjects were included in Cohort 3; however, due to a
dosing error, these subjects were replaced. Excluding the subjects in Cohort 3, a total of 8 subjects were
randomized to Sequences 2 through 6, and 7 subjects were randomized to Sequence 1.

(6.6%) were discontinued. Subjects were discontinued due to AE (1 of 61 subjects
[1.6%]), subject withdrawal (2 of 61 subjects [3.3%]), or other (family issues outside of
the trial [PDATA-2]; 1 of 61 subjects [1.6%]).

2.2. Sponsor’s Analyses of the Pharmacodynamic endpoints
2.2.1. Statistical methodologies used in the sponsor’s analyses
Primary Endpoint Analysis (Drug Liking VAS Emax)
The primary pharmacodynamic endpoint is Emax for “At this Moment” Drug Liking VAS and will be
evaluated with a linear mixed effects model containing treatment, period, sequence, baseline (predose, as
applicable), and
(if applicable) as fixed effects. The subject nested within treatment sequence will
be included as random effect. The first order carryover effect will be included in the model as fixed effect
and will be dropped if not statistically significant at 25% significance level. From this model, least
squares means and two-sided 90% confidence intervals (CIs) will be provided for each treatment;
and difference in least squares mean, two-sided 90% CIs of the difference, and p-values
will be provided for each treatment comparison.
The residuals from the mixed effect model will be investigated for normality using the Shapiro-Wilk test.
Parameters will be analyzed as having a normal distribution if the probability value of the test is not less
than 0.05. Parameters that do not meet this criterion will be analyzed non-parametrically. Overall
treatment effect will be assessed using Friedman’s test. If the distribution of differences between 2
treatments is relatively un-skewed and moderately symmetric, a paired t-test will be used for the pairwise
treatment comparisons; if the distribution of differences between 2 treatments is highly skewed, the
Wilcoxon sign test will be used to test the median difference.
Reviewer’s comments: The Wilcoxon Signed-Rank Test assumes that the distribution of paired differences
is symmetric. It should not be used when the distribution of differences between two treatments is highly
skewed. In the reviewer's analyses, the Sign test was used in such cases.
To assess the abuse potential of centanafadine, the trial validation test will include the
treatment comparisons of all doses of d-amphetamine versus placebo, the relative abuse
potential test will include the comparisons of all doses of centanafadine versus each dose
of d-amphetamine, and the absolute abuse potential test will include the comparisons of
each dose of centanafadine versus placebo. The pairwise comparisons will be derived
from the above described model.
For the contrast to test trial validity, the hypotheses are defined as: 𝐻𝐻𝐻𝐻: 𝜇𝜇𝐶𝐶 − 𝜇𝜇𝑝𝑝 ≤ 10 vs.
𝐻𝐻𝐻𝐻: 𝜇𝜇𝑐𝑐 − 𝜇𝜇𝑝𝑝 > 10, where 𝜇𝜇𝑐𝑐 is mean liking for d-amphetamine and 𝜇𝜇𝑝𝑝 is the mean liking for
placebo. If the null hypothesis is rejected for the 1-sided test (α = 0.05) on the primary
endpoint (Drug Liking VAS Emax), the trial will be considered valid. The margin of 10
was selected based on previous studies of this type and the doses to be administered in
Part 2 Treatment.

10

Reference ID: 5793957

For the contrasts to test relative abuse potential, the hypotheses are defined as:
𝐻𝐻𝐻𝐻: 𝜇𝜇𝑐𝑐 − 𝜇𝜇𝑡𝑡 ≤ 0 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝐻𝐻: 𝜇𝜇𝑐𝑐 − 𝜇𝜇𝑡𝑡 > 0, where 𝜇𝜇𝑡𝑡 is mean liking for centanafadine and 𝜇𝜇𝑐𝑐 is the mean
liking for d-amphetamine, at the 1-sided α level of 0.05. Each dose level of centanafadine will be tested,
compared to all doses of d-amphetamine.
For the contrasts to test absolute abuse potential of centanafadine, the hypotheses are defined as:
𝐻𝐻𝜊𝜊 : 𝜇𝜇𝑡𝑡 − 𝜇𝜇𝑝𝑝 ≥ 11 vs. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝑡𝑡 − 𝜇𝜇𝑝𝑝 < 11, where 𝜇𝜇𝑡𝑡 is mean liking for centanafadine and 𝜇𝜇𝑝𝑝 is the
mean liking for placebo, at the 1-sided α level of 0.05. Each dose level of centanafadine will be tested
against placebo. If the difference between a centanafadine dose and placebo is statistically lower than the
equivalence margin of 11, this will suggest that this dose of centanafadine does not produce an abuserelated signal for Drug Liking VAS. This analysis will be performed using the primary endpoint, Drug
Liking VAS Emax. The margin of 11 was selected based on Chen and Bonson (2013).
For each hypothesis, the statistical significance of the test should be achieved on all doses. Dose-response
will also be examined by evaluating differences in the slope of Emax of Drug Liking for centanafadine
and d-amphetamine.
Key secondary endpoint analyses
The secondary endpoints will be analyzed following the same procedure used in the primary endpoint
analyses. These statistical tests will be performed using one-sided significance level of 5%. No
adjustments will be made for multiple comparisons. Comparisons among treatments for secondary
endpoints will also be evaluated at a 1-sided significance level of 0.05 using the hypotheses shown below.
No specific margins have been selected for secondary endpoints, as there is currently no scientific
literature to support selection of such margins.
The hypotheses tests for the contrast to test trial validity and relative abuse potential will
be performed as follows, respectively:

•

•

For the contrast to test trial validity, 𝐻𝐻𝜊𝜊 : 𝜇𝜇𝑐𝑐 − 𝜇𝜇𝑝𝑝 ≤ 0 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝑐𝑐 − 𝜇𝜇𝑝𝑝 > 0, where 𝜇𝜇𝑐𝑐
is mean response for d-amphetamine and 𝜇𝜇𝑝𝑝 is the mean response for placebo, at the 1sided α level of 0.05
For the contrasts to test relative abuse potential of centanafadine, 𝐻𝐻𝜊𝜊 : 𝜇𝜇𝑐𝑐 − 𝜇𝜇𝑡𝑡 ≤
0 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 ∶ 𝜇𝜇𝑐𝑐 − 𝜇𝜇𝑡𝑡 > 0, where 𝜇𝜇𝑡𝑡 is mean response for centanafadine and 𝜇𝜇𝑐𝑐 is the
mean response for d-amphetamine, at the 1-sided α level of 0.05.

Two-sided 90% CIs will be provided for each treatment; and difference in least squares mean, two-sided
90% CIs of the difference, and p-values will be provided for each treatment comparison.
For the contrast to test absolute abuse potential of test drug, Chen and Tsong (2007) proposed to use mean
response of placebo as margin while comparing test drug and placebo. Chen and Bonson (2013)
suggested 0.67 times standard deviation as margin for the same comparison. However, both these
methods have their limitations and couldn’t be applied for all endpoints. In Chen and Bonson, meta

11

Reference ID: 5793957

analyses were proposed to determine the margin for the comparison on abuse- related subjective
measures. Due to the unavailability of enough historical data, to avoid conducting the tests based on
arbitrarily selected margins, two-sided 90% CIs will be provided for each treatment of centanafadine; and
treatment difference (between centanafadine and placebo) in least squares mean and two-sided 90% CIs
of the difference will be provided for each treatment comparison.
Similarly, the per-protocol analysis will be performed if more than 15% of completers are excluded from
per-protocol pharmacodynamic population; sensitivity analysis will be also conducted for all key
secondary endpoints, excluding those completers with treatment-emergent AE of vomiting during Part 2
Treatment.
2.2.2. Sponsor’s summary and conclusions
The sponsor’s pharmacodynamic results
Primary Endpoint: Drug Liking VAS Emax
•

•

•

•

Mean At This Moment Drug Liking VAS scores for 15 and 30 mg d-amphetamine increased
(indicating drug liking) after dosing and reached a peak at 1.5 hours post-dose. In contrast, mean
scores for 200, 400, and 800 mg centanafadine decreased (indicating drug disliking) after dosing
and reached a maximum decrease at 0.50 hours post-dose before returning to neutral. There was a
small increase (indicating drug liking) in mean scores for 200 and 400 mg centanafadine with
scores reaching a maximum increase at 1.5 and 3 hours post-dose respectively.
Dose response was statistically significant for d-amphetamine (p = 0.0001) but not for
centanafadine (p = 0.7029) for At This Moment Drug Liking VAS Emax, indicating that taking

a higher dose of centanafadine, in contrast to d-amphetamine, does not result in increased
drug liking.
Each dose of centanafadine showed significantly lower At This Moment Drug Liking VAS
scores (2-sided p-value < 0.0001) compared with 30 mg d-amphetamine for Emax,
confirming less abuse potential of centanafadine compared with 30 mg d-amphetamine.

The dose of 800 mg centanafadine showed a significantly lower At This Moment Drug Liking
VAS score (2-sided p-value = 0.056, 1-sided p-value 0.028 < prespecified alpha of 0.05)
compared with 15 mg d-amphetamine. The dose of 200 mg centanafadine (2-sided p-value =
0.1073) and 400 mg centanafadine (2-sided p-value = 0.1602) were numerically lower,

though not statistically significant when compared with 15 mg d-amphetamine for Emax.

•

At This Moment Drug Liking VAS Emin scores were significantly lower (2-sided p-value <
0.0001) for centanafadine when compared with both d-amphetamine and placebo, indicating a

substantial degree of disliking.

Key Secondary Endpoints: Overall Drug Liking VAS Emax and Take Drug Again VAS Emax

12

Reference ID: 5793957

•

Both 15 mg d-amphetamine (2-sided p-value = 0.0004 or less) and 30 mg d-amphetamine (2-

sided p-value < 0.0001) showed significantly higher scores compared with all doses of
centanafadine for the Overall Drug Liking VAS Emax. The doses of 400 mg
centanafadine (2-sided p-value = 0.0202) and 800 mg centanafadine (2-sided p-value <
0.0001) were significantly lower compared with placebo, implying significant disliking.
Both 15 and 30 mg d-amphetamine (2-sided p-value < 0.0001) showed significantly
higher scores compared with all doses of centanafadine for the Take Drug Again VAS
Emax. The dose of 800 mg centanafadine had a similar score to placebo (2-sided p-value
= 0.1820) whereas both 200 and 400 mg showed significantly higher scores compared
with placebo (2-sided p-value = 0.0336 or less).
Negative Endpoints (Bad Effects VAS Emax and Nausea VAS Emax)
•

The negative endpoints revealed distinct aversive effects of centanafadine compared with damphetamine and placebo. While d-amphetamine showed either significant but modest
differences or no differences from placebo, centanafadine was associated with statistically greater
negative effects.

Conclusion
Overall, the results of this study strongly suggest a lower degree of abuse potential for centanafadine
compared with the positive control, d-amphetamine.

The mean (SD) Drug Liking VAS Emax scores were 85.1 (11.4) for d-amphetamine 30 mg (median = 84)
and 64.7 (21.2) for the high dose of centanafadine (median = 57). The median TEmax values were 1.5

Reference ID: 5793957

The peak mean responses for d-amphetamine were 70.0 (15 mg) and 80.6 (30 mg), both occurring at 1.5
hours post-dose. For centanafadine, the peak mean responses were 61.1 at 1.5 hours (200 mg), 56.6 at 3
hours (400 mg), and 48.3 at 2.5 hours (800 mg) post-dose. Drug liking scores showed a dose-dependent
decrease with increasing centanafadine doses, and all mean response curves showed a dip at 0.5 hours.
Notably, the entire response profiles for both the 400 mg and 800 mg centanafadine doses remained
below 56.6 throughout the observation period.
2.4.1.2. Statistical testing
Primary Endpoint: Drug Liking VAS Emax
For the primary endpoint, the study must demonstrate three key criteria:
1. Study Validation: At least one dose of d-amphetamine must have significantly larger mean
responses than placebo, with a test value of 15, to validate the study. (The sponsor used margin of
10 for this comparison).
2. Relative Abuse Potential: The mean difference between the positive control and the test drug
must be significantly greater than 0, demonstrating that all doses of centanafadine have reduced
drug liking effects compared to d-amphetamine.
3. Placebo Similarity: all doses of centanafadine must demonstrate a mean difference from placebo
that is significantly less than 11, showing that drug liking for centanafadine is similar to placebo.
Statistical hypotheses
Test 1 (Study validation):

𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝐶𝐶15 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 ≤ 15 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝐶𝐶15 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 > 15

𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝐶𝐶30 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 ≤ 15 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝐶𝐶30 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 > 15

Test 2 (Relative abuse potential): 𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝐶𝐶15 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇200 ≤ 0 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝐶𝐶15 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇200 >0
𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝐶𝐶15 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇400 ≤ 0 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝐶𝐶15 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇400 > 0

𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝐶𝐶15 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇800 ≤ 0 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝐶𝐶15 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇800 >0

𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝐶𝐶30 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇200 ≤ 0 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝐶𝐶30 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇200 > 0

𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝐶𝐶30 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇400 ≤ 0 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝐶𝐶30 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇400 >0

𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝐶𝐶30 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇800 ≤ 0 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝐶𝐶30 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇800 >0

Test 3 (Placebo Similarity): 𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇200 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 ≥ 11 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇200 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 < 11

𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇400 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 ≥ 11 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇400 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 < 11

Testing procedure

𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇800 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 ≥ 11 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇800 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 < 11

19

Reference ID: 5793957

This follows a sequential testing approach. An insignificant test result will terminate testing of subsequent
hypotheses. Therefore, no multiplicity adjustment for Type I error rate is required, and the significance
level for each test is 5%.
For the mixed effects model including period, sequence, treatment, and carryover effect as fixed effects
and subject as a random effect, the normality assumption was not met (p = 0.00136) for Drug Liking VAS
Emax. Consequently, the normality and skewness of the paired treatment differences were evaluated. The
paired t-test was used for the cases that p-value of the W test is greater than 0.05 or skewness values fall
between -0.5 and 0.5 (inclusive), and the Sign test otherwise. Table 2 shows results from the primary
analysis of Drug Liking VAS Emax.

The results from the primary analysis of Drug Liking VAS Emax show:
1. The mean difference between each dose of d-amphetamine and placebo was significantly greater
than the test value 15 (p ≤ 0.0036). Thus, the study is validated.
2. The mean (or median) difference between each dose of d-amphetamine and each dose of
centanafadine were significantly greater than zero (p ≤ 0.0403) except the comparison between damphetamine 15 mg and centanafadine 800 mg (p = 0.1620).
3. Due to the result from the comparison between d-amphetamine 15 mg and centanafadine 800 mg,
based on the testing procedure, the comparisons between centanafadine and placebo can only be
considered exploratory. The test results show that the mean (or median) difference between each
dose of centanafadine and placebo was not significantly smaller than the test value 11 (p ≥
0.3220).

2.4.2. Analyses on Overall Drug Liking VAS Emax and Take Drug Again VAS Emax

treatment period.

The mean and median Overall Drug Liking VAS Emax scores were below 60 for centanafadine 200 mg
and below 50 for centanafadine 400 and 800 mg. The mean and median differences between
centanafadine 400 and 800 mg and placebo were negative. This indicates that on average, subjects overall
disliked medium and high doses of centanafadine compared to placebo.
Take Drug Again VAS was on a unipolar scale in this study. It was not clearly identified in the protocol
or SAP but can be determined based on the mean and median response from placebo. The mean scores
produced by each dose of centanafadine were at least 30.9 and 42.1 points lower than those produced by
15 and 30 mg of d-amphetamine, respectively. The median differences between 400 and 800 mg doses of

The significant level was 0.05 for the comparisons between d-amphetamine and placebo, and between damphetamine and centanafadine, and was 0.10 for the comparisons between centanafadine and placebo.
The same statistical model used in the primary analysis was used for both Overall Drug Liking VAS
Emax and Take Drug Again Emax.
For Overall Drug Liking VAS Emax, the normal assumption of the mixed effects model was not met (p =
0.0266). Consequently, the normality and skewness of the paired treatment differences were evaluated.
The paired t-test was used for the cases that p-value of the W test is greater than 0.05 or skewness values
fall between -0.5 and 0.5 (inclusive), and the Sign test otherwise. Table 5 shows results from the analysis
on Overall Drug Liking VAS Emax.

The reviewer's secondary analysis shows that for Overall Drug Liking VAS Emax:

1. The mean (or median) difference between each dose of d-amphetamine and placebo was
significantly greater than 0 (p ≤ 0.0036).
2. The mean (or median) difference between each dose of d-amphetamine and each dose of
centanafadine was significantly greater than 0 (p < 0.0001).
3. The mean difference between centanafadine 200 mg and placebo was not significantly different
from 0 (p = 0.1908), while the mean (or median) difference between centanafadine 400 mg and
800 mg and placebo was significantly different from 0 (p ≤ 0.0860). Note that the mean
difference between centanafadine 400 mg and placebo was -7.7, and the median difference
between centanafadine 800 mg and placebo was -27.5, indicating that overall, subjects did not
like the 400 mg and 800 mg centanafadine doses compared to placebo.
For Take Drug Again VAS Emax, the normality assumption of the mixed effects model was met (p =
0.1211). The Levene test on the residuals was significant (p = 0.0383). The carryover effect was not
significant (p = 0.0567), but the p-value was less than 0.25; therefore, the carryover effect was included as
a fixed effect in the model. Table 6 shows the analysis results.

The descriptive statistics for the other 7 VAS Emax secondary endpoints (Any Drug Effects, Bad Drug

2.4.4. Summary Statistics for Drug Similarity VAS
The Drug Similarity VAS provides an estimate of drug class with which drug users identify the test drug.
The same VAS procedures used to assess subjective drug responses were used to assess perceived drug
similarity. Subjects were asked to compare the drug they received to a comparison drug on a scale ranging
from “Not at all similar” to “Very similar”. Each scale was scored as an integer from 0 to 100,
where the position of the subject’s response on the scale represented a defined integer. Drugs a subject
has not personally experienced often enough to use as a standard of comparison (based on the screening
assessment) were not included in his or her questionnaire.
Table 8 displays summary statistics for three choices of drugs (Cocaine, d-amphetamine, and ecstasy) that
produced larger means compared to other choice of drugs for each treatment for Drug Similarity VAS.

The number of subjects providing similarity ratings for each drug differed because the similarity question
was only asked of subjects who had prior experience with that specific drug. The study subjects were
healthy adult recreational drug users with stimulant experience. Forty subjects had experience with damphetamine.
Based on Drug Similarity VAS, subjects on average rated 15 and 30 mg d-amphetamine as most similar
to d-amphetamine. On average, subjects rated centanafadine 200 mg as more similar to d-amphetamine
than the 400 and 800 mg doses of centanafadine. However, the actual similarity scores tell a more
nuanced story: the mean and median scores for centanafadine 200 mg were 30.3 and 24, respectively,
compared to 51.8 and 57 for d-amphetamine 15 mg, and 71 and 74.5 for d-amphetamine 30 mg.

The descriptive statistics based on ARCI A and ARCI BG show that the stimulant effects produced by

response or an inverse dose response.

3. Conclusion
Based on 42 completers in the Agency-recommended modified completer population, the results from the
reviewer's primary analysis on Drug Liking VAS Emax and secondary analyses on the key secondary
endpoints (Overall Drug Liking VAS Emax and Take Drug Again VAS Emax) demonstrated that
centanafadine has less abuse potential than d-amphetamine.
Analyses of 7 additional secondary endpoints—VAS Emax scores of Any Drug Effects, Bad Drug
Effects, Good Drug Effects, High, Nausea, Agitation/Relaxation, Alertness/Drowsiness and Drug
Similarity as well as two ARCI Emax scores of A and BG supported this finding.

1. Background
In this submission the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in rats
and one in mice. These studies were conducted to evaluate the oncogenic potential of
Centanafadine (EB-1020), a triple reuptake inhibitor for the treatment of ADHD, when
administered once daily by oral gavage for up to 84 weeks in males and 91 weeks in females of
CD® [Crl:CD®(SD)] rats, and for a minimum of 26 weeks to CByB6F1/Tg rasH2 hemizygous
mice.
In this review the phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component (trend) of the
effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor
incidence rate as dose increases.
2. Rat Study
Two separate experiments, one in male rats and one in female rats were conducted. As indicated
in Table 1, in the experiment there were three treated groups, and one vehicle control group. Two
hundred and fourty CD® [Crl:CD®(SD)] rats of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 60
rats per group. Rats in Groups 2 through 4 received test article by oral gavage at a daily dose of
3, 7, and 20 mg/kg/day for male rats and 3, 10, and 30 mg/kg/day for female rats at a volume of
10 mL/kg. In this review the three treated groups were referred to as the low (Group 2), mid
(Group 3), and high (Group 4) dose groups, respectively. The rats in the vehicle control group
(Group 1) were administrated distilled water, and handled for the same duration and in the same
manner as the treated groups.
Due to achievement of survival criteria, surviving males were terminated at
Weeks 83 to 84 and females were terminated at Week 91.

The following parameters and end points were evaluated in this study: mortality, clinical
observations, body weight, food consumption, hematology, toxicokinetic (TK) parameters for
EB-1020 and EB-1020 lactam (also known as EB-10601), gross necropsy findings, and
histopathologic examinations.
2.1.
2.1.1.

Reference ID: 5794246

Survival analysis

Sponsor's analyses

NDA 218145 / IND 119361 (EB-1020)

Page 4

In the sponsor’s report, intercurrent mortality data were analyzed using the Kaplan-Meier
product-limit method. An overall test comparing all groups was conducted using a log-rank test.
If this overall test was significant (p<0.05) and there were more than two groups, then a follow
up analysis was done where each treatment group was compared to the control group using a logrank test. Results of all pair-wise comparisons are reported at the 0.05 and 0.01 significance
levels. All endpoints were analyzed using two tailed tests.
Sponsor’s findings:
The sponsor’s analysis showed that the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy were
20 (33%), 23 (38%), 25 (42%), and 28 (47%) in the vehicle control, low, mid, and high dose
groups, respectively for male rats, and 15 (25%), 17 (28%), 14 (23%), and 13 (22%) in the vehicle
control, low, mid, and high dose groups, respectively for female rats. For both males and females,
there were no statistically significant differences in mortality amongst the groups.
2.1.2.

Tumor data analysis

In the sponsor’s analysis, tumor incidence data were analyzed using both survival adjusted and
unadjusted tests. The unadjusted tests were based on the incidence and number of sites examined
for each tumor type. The Cochran-Armitage trend test was calculated for testing positive trend
(dose response) and Fisher’s exact test was used to compare each treatment group with the
control group. The trend test was applied for all control and treatment groups and was arranged
in a strictly increasing dose level. The survival adjusted test was conducted according to the
prevalence/mortality methods described by Peto et al.. The Poly-1 method was used to assess
prevalence of tumors. The survival-adjusted rates based on the risk weights are displayed. The
tests of significance included both an overall trend and pair-wise comparisons of each treatment
group with the control. All p-values are reported using upper-tailed tests, unless otherwise
indicated.
Adjustment for multiple testing:
In the sponsor’s report, evaluation criteria (p-values of significance) were applied differently for
rare tumors (background rate of 1% or less) and common tumors (background rate greater than
1%). The evaluation criteria (from the FDA) are given in Table 2.
Table 2. Evaluation Criteria for Common and Rare Tumors

Sponsor’s findings:
In the sponsor’s report, there were no test article-related neoplastic or non-neoplastic findings.
In females at 3 mg/kg/day, but not at higher doses, one of the pairwise statistical assessments
(Poly-1 pairwise) was significant (P=0.0080) for pituitary gland adenomas; however, all other
statistical tests (Fischer’s exact test, Cochran-Armitage trend test, Peto overall test, and Poly-1
overall trend test) were not statistically significant. Further support for the lack of a test article

Reference ID: 5794246

NDA 218145 / IND 119361 (EB-1020)

Page 5

induction in pituitary adenomas in low dose females included a lack of a dose response (77%,
92%, 75%, and 73% incidence in females at 0, 3, 10, and 30 mg/kg/day, respectively), similarity
of the incidence with historical control data, lack of any statistically significant findings in male
pituitary gland adenomas, and lack of any dose-related increase in preneoplastic proliferative
pituitary gland findings such as focal or diffuse pituitary gland hyperplasia. Therefore, the
isolated statistically significant Poly-1 pairwise test for pituitary gland adenomas in females at
3 mg/kg/day was considered incidental and not Centanafadine (EB-1020) related.
The microscopic findings noted in test article-treated rats were considered incidental background
findings. These findings were of the nature commonly observed in mature CD[Crl:CD(SD)] rats,
and/or were of similar incidence and severity amongst control and test article-treated animals.
Thus, they were considered incidental/spontaneous and not test article related.
2.2.

Reviewer's analyses

To verify the sponsor’s analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing
toxicologist, this reviewer independently performed the survival and tumor data analyses using
the data provided by the sponsor electronically.
2.2.1.

Survival analysis

In the reviewer’s analysis, the survival distributions of rats in all four groups (Groups 1, 2, 3, and 4)
were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. The dose response relationship was
tested across Groups 1, 2, 3, and 4 using the likelihood ratio test, and the homogeneity of survival
distributions was tested using the log-rank test.
Reviewer’s findings:
The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rates are given in Figures 1A and 1B in the appendix for all
four groups in male and female rats, respectively. The intercurrent mortality data of all four groups
and the results of the tests for dose response relationship and homogeneity of survivals for Groups
1, 2, 3, and 4 are given in Tables 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
The reviewer’s analysis showed that the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy
were 20 (33%), 23 (38%), 25 (42%), and 28 (47%) in the vehicle control low, mid, and high dose
groups, respectively for male rats, and 15 (25%), 17 (28%), 14 (23%), and 13 (22%) in the vehicle
control low, mid, and high dose groups, respectively for for female rats. No statistically significant
dose response relationship or pairwise comparisons in mortality were observed for either male or
female rats.
2.2.2.

Tumor data analysis

The tumor data were analyzed for dose response relationships across the vehicle control, low, mid,
and high dose groups, and pairwise comparisons of each of the three treated groups against the
vehicle control group, using the Poly-k method described in the paper of Bailer and Portier (1988)
and Bieler and Williams (1993).

Reference ID: 5794246

NDA 218145 / IND 119361 (EB-1020)

Page 6

In the ploy-k method, the adjustment for differences in mortality among treatment groups is
made by modifying the number of animals at risk in the denominators in the calculations of
overall tumor rates in the Cochran-Armitage test to reflect less-than-whole-animal contributions
for animals that die without tumor before the end of the study (Bailer and Portier 1988). The
modification is made by defining a new number of animals at risk for each treatment group. The
*
*
number of animals at risk for the i-th treatment group R i is defined as R i = ∑ W ij where w ij
is the weight for the j-th animal in the i-th treatment group, and the sum is over all animals in the
group.
Bailer and Portier (1988) proposed the weight w ij as follows:
wij = 1 to animals dying with the tumor, and
wij = ( tij / tsacr )k to animals dying without the tumor,
where tij is the time of death of the j-th animal in the i-th treatment group, and tsacr is the
planned (or intended) time of terminal sacrifice. The above formulas imply that animals living up
to the end of the planned terminal sacrifice date without developing any tumor will also be
assigned wij =1 since tij = tsacr. Also animals developed the tumor type being tested before the
end of the study will be assigned as wij = 1.
Certain treatment groups of a study or the entire study may be terminated earlier than the planned
(or intended) time of terminal sacrifice due to excessive mortalities. However, based on the
principle of the Intention-to-treat (ITT) analysis in randomized trials, the tsacr should not be
affected by the unplanned early terminations. The tsacr should always be equal to the planned (or
intended) time of terminal sacrifice. For those animals that were sacrificed later than tsacr,
regardless their actual terminal sacrifice time, tsacr was used as their time of terminal sacrifice in
the analysis.
One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends on the
tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For long term 104 week standard rat and mouse
studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis
of this data.
Multiple testing adjustment:
For a submission with one two-year study in one species and one short-term study with another
species, in order to keep the overall false-positive rate at the nominal level of approximately 10%,
as indicated in Table 3, this reviewer used the methodologies suggested in the Expanded
Statistical Decision Rules for Interpretations of Results of Rodent Carcinogenicity Studies of
Pharmaceuticals by Lin and Rahman (2018).
A rare tumor is defined as one in which the published spontaneous tumor rate is less than 1%.
However, if the background information for the common or rare tumor is not available, the number
of animals bearing tumors in the vehicle control group in the present study was used to determine
the common or rare tumor status in the review report. That is, if the number of animals bearing
tumors in the vehicle control group is 0, then this tumor is considered as the rare tumor; otherwise,
if the number of animals bearing tumors in the control group is greater than or equal to 1, then this
tumor is considered as the common tumor.

Reference ID: 5794246

NDA 218145 / IND 119361 (EB-1020)

Page 7

Table 3. Recommended decision rules for controlling the overall false positive rates

Reviewer’s findings:
The tumor rates and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 2A and 2B in the
appendix for male and female rats, respectively. Based on the criteria of adjustment for multiple
testing described above, no statistically significant dose response relationship or pairwise
comparisons were observed in the tumor data for either male or female rats.
3. Mouse Study
Two separate experiments, one in male mice and one in female mice were conducted. As
indicated in Table 4, in the experiment there were three treated groups, one vehicle control
group, and one positive control group. One hundred and ten transgenic CByB6F1/Tg rasH2
hemizygous mice of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 25 mice per group except for the
positive control group which contains 10 mice. EB-1020 was given via oral gavage once daily, at
a dose of 0 (vehicle control, Group 1), 1, 3, and 10 mg/kg/dose at a volume of 10 mL/kg/dose for
up to 26 weeks for both male and female mice. In this review the three treated groups were
referred to as the low (Group 2), mid (Group 3), and high (Group 4) dose groups, respectively.
The mice in the vehicle control group (Group 1) were administrated with Reverse Osmosis
Deionized (RODI) water, and handled for the same duration and in the same manner as the
treated groups. The positive control group (Group 5) was administered N-Nitrosomethylurea

Reference ID: 5794246

NDA 218145 / IND 119361 (EB-1020)

Page 8

(NMU) only once on Day 1 via intraperitoneal injection. These animals were included as
positive controls to ensure animals supplied appropriately express oncogenes and respond to
carcinogenic insult.

Assessment of carcinogenicity was based on mortality, clinical observations, body weights, mass
observations, food consumption, and clinical and anatomic pathology. Blood samples were
collected for toxicokinetic evaluation.
3.1.

Sponsor's analyses

Because the mouse study was conducted by the same testing facility as the rat study, the sponsor
used the same methodologies that used for the analyses of the rat survival and tumor data.
3.1.1.

Survival analysis

In the sponsor’s report, the statistical analyses of mortality main data, excluding the positive
control group (Group 5), were performed separately for each sex. The survival function of each
group was estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product-limit method (SAS Institute, 2016) applied
on daily intervals and graphs were produced. The log-rank test was applied to Groups 1 to 4 in
order to assess the significance of the overall group effect on mortality data. If this test was
found to be significant, then the significance of an overall dose-related trend in mortality across
the control article group (Group 1) and the 3 test article-treated groups (Groups 2, 3, and 4) was
evaluated using Peto’s two-sided test (Peto, 1980). In addition, the mortality rate in Group 1 was
compared to the one in each of Groups 2, 3, and 4 using Peto’s two-sided test.
Sponsor’s findings:
The sponsor’s analysis showed that the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy
were 24 (96%), 25 (100%), 24 (96%), 24 (96%), and 4 (27%) in the vehicle control low, mid,
high dose , and the positive control groups, respectively for male mice, and 25 (100%), 23 (92%),
24 (96%), 24 (96%), and 2 (13%) in the vehicle control low, mid, high dose, and the positive
control groups, respectively for female mice. No statistically significant dose response
relationship and pairwise comparisons in mortality was reported for either male or female mice.
3.1.2.

Reference ID: 5794246

Tumor data analysis

NDA 218145 / IND 119361 (EB-1020)

Page 9

In the sponsor’s report, the statistical evaluation of tumor data, excluding the positive control
group (Group 5), was done separately for each sex and was limited to subcutis and
hemolymphoreticular tissue using all study animals, to all non-secondary neoplastic lesions
found in protocol-required tissues/sites, and to the combination of hemangiosarcoma findings
across whole body. At the discretion of the Study Pathologist and with sponsor agreement, some
relevant additional combinations of neoplastic findings were required. These additional
combinations were detailed in a signed memo provided by the Study Pathologist and approved
by the sponsor prior to the statistical analysis. For each dataset of interest within each sex, the
significance of an overall linear dose-related increase in tumor incidence rate, across Groups 1,
2, 3, and 4, was evaluated using Cochran-Armitage’s one-sided exact test. Furthermore, Group 1
was compared to each of Groups 2, 3, and 4. These pairwise comparisons were implemented
using Fisher’s exact one-sided test, in order to check if the tumor incidence rate in each of
Groups 2, 3, and 4 was significantly higher than the tumor incidence rate in Group 1.
Multiple testing adjustment:
Sponsor’s findings:
In the sponsor’s report, the incidence of tumors noted in this study with Centanafadine was not
considered tumorigenic within the context of this assay. No neoplastic findings had (or showed)
a statistically significant increase in Centanafadine-treated mice relative to vehicle controls for
common or rare tumors for pair wise comparisons (Fisher’s exact test) or trend tests (CochranArmitage overall trend test). The positive control group had tumors induced by NMU, supporting
the validity of the assay.
3.2.

Reviewer's analyses

Similar to the rat study, this reviewer independently performed survival and tumor data analyses of
mouse data to verify sponsor’s analyses. Data used in this reviewer's analyses were provided by the
sponsor electronically.
For the analysis of both the survival data and the tumor data in mice, this reviewer used similar
methodologies that were used for the analyses of the rat survival and tumor data.
3.2.1.

Survival analysis

Reviewer’s findings:
The reviewer’s analysis showed that the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy
were 24 (96%), 25 (100%), 24 (96%), 24 (96%), and 4 (27%) in the vehicle control group, the
low, mid, and high dose groups, respectively for male mice, and 25 (100%), 23 (92%), 24 (96%),
24 (96%), and 2 (13%), respectively for female mice. No statistically significant findings in
mortality were noted in either male or female mice.
3.2.2.

Reference ID: 5794246

Tumor data analysis

NDA 218145 / IND 119361 (EB-1020)

Page 10

Reviewer’s findings:
The tumor rates and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 4A and 4B in the
appendix for male and female mice, respectively.
In the reviewer’s analysis, no statistically significant dose response relationship or pairwise
comparisons in any tested tumor was noted in either male or female mice. For both male and
female mice, the pairwise compairons between the positive control group (Group 5) and the
vehicle control group (Group 1) showed p-values less than 0.05 for many tumors, confirming
animal expressed oncogenes and responded to carcinogenic insult as expected.
4. Summary
In this submission the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in rats
and one in mice. These studies were conducted to evaluate the oncogenic potential of
Centanafadine (EB-1020), a triple reuptake inhibitor for the treatment of ADHD, when
administered once daily by oral gavage for up to 84 weeks in males and 91 weeks in females of
CD® [Crl:CD®(SD)] rats, and when given orally for a minimum of 26 weeks to CByB6F1/Tg
rasH2 hemizygous mice.
Rat Study:
Two separate experiments, one in male rats and one in female rats were conducted. In each of the
experiment there were three treated groups, and one vehicle control group. Two hundred and
fourty CD® [Crl:CD®(SD)] rats of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 60 rats per
group. Rats in Groups 2 through 4 received test article by oral gavage at a daily doses of 3, 7,
and 20 mg/kg/day for male rats and 3, 10, and 30 mg/kg/day for female rats at a volume of 10
mL/kg. Due to achievement of survival criteria, surviving males were terminated at
Weeks 83 to 84 and main study females were terminated at Week 91.
The reviewer’s analysis showed that the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy
were 20 (33%), 23 (38%), 25 (42%), and 28 (47%) in the vehicle control low, mid, high dose
groups, respectively for male rats, and 15 (25%), 17 (28%), 14 (23%), and 13 (22%) in the vehicle
control low, mid, high dose groups, respectively for female rats. No statistically significant dose
response relationship or pairwise differences in mortality were observed in either male or female
rats.
In this reviewer’s analysis, no statistically significant dose response relationship or pairwise
differences were observed in the tumor data for either male or female rats.
Mouse Study:
Two separate experiments, one in male mice and one in female mice were conducted. In each of
the experiment there were three treated groups, one vehicle control group, and one positive
control group. One hundred and ten transgenic CByB6F1/Tg rasH2 hemizygous mice of each sex
were assigned randomly in size of 25 mice per group except for the positive control group which

Reference ID: 5794246

NDA 218145 / IND 119361 (EB-1020)

Page 11

contains 10 mice. Study animals were given EB-1020 via oral gavage once daily, at a dose of 0
(vehicle control, Group 1), 1, 3, and 10 mg/kg/dose at a volume of 10 mL/kg/dose for up to 26
weeks for both male and female mice.
The reviewer’s analysis showed that the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy
were 24 (96%), 25 (100%), 24 (96%), 24 (96%), and 4 (27%) in the vehicle control low, mid, and
high dose groups, respectively for male mice, and 25 (100%), 23 (92%), 24 (96%), 24 (96%), and
2 (13%) in the vehicle control low, mid, and high dose groups, respectively for female mice. No
statistically significant findings in mortality were noted in either male or female mice.
In this reviewer’s analysis, no statistically significant dose response relationship or pairwise
differences in any tested tumor was noted in either male or female mice. For both male and
female mice, the pairwise compairons between the positive control group (Group 5) and the
vehicle control group (Group 1) showed p-values less than 0.05 for many tumors, confirming
animal expressed oncogenes and responded to carcinogenic insult as expected.

1. Executive Summary
The applicant, Otsuka Pharmaceutical, submitted the report for Study EB-1020-IR-103, a single-dose,
randomized, double-blind, placebo- and active-controlled crossover study. The study evaluated the
abuse potential of single oral doses of EB-1020 IR at 400 mg and 800 mg, compared with damphetamine (d-AMP; 40 mg), lisdexamfetamine (LDX; 150 mg), and placebo in healthy recreational
stimulant users.
Study Population
A total of 109 subjects were screened for enrollment, and 83 (76.1%) were randomized to the
Qualification Phase. Of these 83 subjects, 39 (47.0%) met the qualification criteria. A total of 36
subjects were randomized to the Treatment Phase and received at least one dose of study drug. Thirty
(83.3%) subjects completed all five treatment periods, and 26 subjects were included in the Modified
Completer Population (MCP).
Primary Endpoint Results
For the primary endpoint (Drug Liking VAS Emax), both d-AMP and LDX produced significantly
greater drug liking (>15 points) compared to placebo, thus validating the study.
The primary analysis of Drug Liking VAS Emax did not demonstrate that EB-1020 was significantly
less liked by subjects compared to both d-AMP and LDX, except for the comparison between LDX
and EB-1020 400 mg. Due to the results from the comparison between the positive controls and each
dose of EB-1020, and based on the testing procedure, the comparison between each dose of EB-1020
and placebo was not performed. In fact, the mean difference between each dose of EB-1020 and
placebo (>11, which exceeds the test value) clearly indicates that the test drug is not similar to
placebo.
Individual time course response profiles showed that 65.4% of subjects disliked EB-1020 800 mg at
0.25 or 0.5 hours post-dose. In some subjects, this disliking persisted for a prolonged period, while
others showed liking starting at 1 hour post-dose.
Secondary Endpoint Results
The secondary analysis of Emax of Overall Drug Liking VAS did not demonstrate that subjects
overall significantly less liked EB-1020 compared to d-AMP and LDX, except for the comparison
between LDX and EB-1020 400 mg. An exploratory analysis showed that Overall liking produced by
both doses of EB-1020 was significantly greater than placebo.
Results from the analysis of Emax of Take Drug Again VAS did not demonstrate that subjects were
significantly less willing to take EB-1020 again compared to both d-AMP and LDX, except for the
comparison between LDX and EB-1020 800 mg. An exploratory analysis showed that subjects were
more willing to take EB-1020 again compared to placebo.

4

Reference ID: 5793951

Descriptive statistics for the other seven VAS Emax secondary endpoints (Any Drug Effects, Bad
Drug Effects, Good Drug Effects, High, Alertness/Drowsiness, Agitation/Relaxation, and Nausea)
showed that compared to both d-AMP and LDX, EB-1020 produced larger mean and median for good
effects at 800 mg dose and similar (vs. LDX) to greater (vs. d-AMP) euphoric effects at the 400 mg
dose and higher euphoric effects at the 800 mg dose (vs. both d-AMP and LDX). However, EB-1020
produced greater bad effects and nausea at both doses compared to the positive controls. Analyses of
Drug Similarity VAS and two ARCI Emax scores (A and BG) showed that EB-1020 had less
stimulant effect compared to d-AMP and LDX.
Since only 26 subjects were included in the MCP in this study, the power of statistical tests was low.
Conclusion
Study EB-1020-IR-103 did not demonstrate that EB-1020 has less abuse potential than either d-AMP
or LDX.

Reference ID: 5793951

2. Review report on Study EB-1020-IR-103
2.1. Overview
Study EB-1020-IR-103 was a single-dose, randomized, double-blind, placebo- and active-controlled
crossover study to evaluate the abuse potential of EB-1020 IR compared to placebo-, d-amphetamine (dAMP), and lisdexamfetamine (LDX) in healthy recreational stimulant users.
2.1.1. Objectives of the study
Primary objective

•

To evaluate the abuse potential of single doses of EB-1020 IR compared with placebo, damphetamine (d-AMP), and lisdexamfetamine (LDX) in healthy recreational stimulant
users.

Secondary Objective

•

To evaluate the safety and tolerability of EB-1020 IR in healthy recreational stimulant
users.

2.1.2. Study design
This was a single-center, single-dose, randomized, double-blind, placebo- and active-controlled crossover
study with a single inpatient treatment visit. The abuse potential of single oral doses of EB-1020 IR (400
mg, 800 mg) was compared with that of placebo, d-AMP (40 mg; active control), and LDX (150 mg;
active control) in healthy recreational stimulant users. Subjects participated in a medical Screening visit
(Visit 1), one 4-day inpatient Qualification Phase (Visit 2), one 11-day inpatient Treatment Phase (Visit
3), and a safety Follow-up visit (Visit 4).
Following qualification, eligible subjects were randomized to the inpatient Treatment Phase. Subjects
received each of the following 5 oral treatments in a randomized, double-blind, crossover manner
following an overnight fast:

•
•
•
•
•

Treatment A: EB-1020 400 mg
Treatment B: EB-1020 800 mg
Treatment C: d-AMP 40 mg
Treatment D: LDX 150 mg
Treatment E: Placebo

The washout period between treatment periods was approximately 48 hours. A Follow-up visit was
conducted approximately 3 to 5 days following discharge from the Treatment Phase or after early
discontinuation from the study. Each subject participated in the study for up to approximately 6 weeks,
from Screening to Follow-up. The study design schematic is presented in Figure 1.

6

Reference ID: 5793951

Figure 1: Study Design Schematic

Note: This figure is from Figure 1 in the study report.

2.1.3. Analysis populations
The study analysis populations were defined as follows:
• Randomized population: All subjects who were assigned a randomization number in the
Treatment Phase
• Safety population: All randomized subjects who received any study drug in the Treatment Phase
• Pharmacodynamic population in the Treatment Phase: All randomized subjects who completed
all study treatment periods in the Treatment Phase and who had no major protocol deviations that

7

Reference ID: 5793951

•
•

would have impacted PD results
Pharmacodynamic population in the Qualification Phase: All randomized subjects who completed
all study treatment periods in the Qualification Phase and had no major protocol deviations that
would have impacted PD results.

Reviewer’s comments: The Pharmacodynamic Population is the Completer Population without major
protocol deviations. The reviewer’s analyses are based on the Modified Completer Population (MCP)
defined as follows:
Modified Completer Population: All subjects in the Completer Population, excluding subjects with the
following elimination criteria: for Drug Liking VAS,

– Emax for d-amphetamine 30 mg ≤55, or
– Emax for placebo – Emax for d-amphetamine 30 mg ≥5, or
– Similar Emax scores (within 5 points difference) across all study treatments
(including placebo).
This population served as the primary population for the pharmacodynamic analyses.
2.1.4. Disposition of subjects
A total of 109 subjects were screened for enrollment and 83 (76.1%) subjects were randomized to the
Qualification Phase (data on file). Of the 83 subjects, 39 (47.0%) met the qualification criteria. The
remaining subjects were qualification failures, discontinued due to subject’s choice or administrative
reasons. A total of 36 subjects were randomized to the Treatment Phase and received at least 1 dose of
study drug. Thirty (83.3%) subjects completed all 5 treatment periods, and 28 (77.8%) subjects completed
the study. Eight (22.2%) subjects discontinued prior to completing the study. Two (5.6%) subjects were
discontinued due to an AE, both reported following administration of LDX 150 mg; 6 (16.7%) subjects
were discontinued due to other reasons, with 4 subjects withdrawing consent and 2 subjects lost to followup.
Reviewer’s comments:
The study had 30 completers, not 28. Two subjects were lost to follow-up but had completed all
assessments before discontinuing, so they should be counted as completers since they finished all
required assessments.
2.1.5. Major protocol deviations

A total of 278 protocol deviations were reported during the Treatment Phase and impacted data
of 35 subjects. All of the deviations were related to Drug Similarity VAS data entry
errors/omissions on the PD assessment platform by clinic staff. Therefore, subjects were not
asked to rate their experience on these Drug Similarity VAS at various prespecified time points
during the Treatment Phase. This deviation resulted in missing data for several subjects across
treatment periods, thereby having a significant impact on the outcome and interpretability of the
Drug Similarity VAS measures.
8

Reference ID: 5793951

Reviewer’s comments: Based on this protocol deviation, Drug Similarity VAS was included in
reviewer’s analysis.

Reviewer’s comments: Only Overall Drug Liking Emax and Take Drug Again Emax will be considered as
key secondary endpoints in the reviewer’s analyses. Other endpoint points on the list will be considered
as secondary endpoints. In addition, because of the major protocol deviation on Drug Similarity VAS, this
endpoint will not be included in the reviewer’s analysis.
2.2. Sponsor’s Analyses of the Pharmacodynamic endpoints
2.2.1. Statistical methodologies used in the sponsor’s analyses
Pharmacodynamic values at each time point were summarized by treatment using descriptive statistics
and presented graphically. Derived endpoints were summarized using descriptive statistics. Outliers were
listed by measure and parameter. Qualification Phase data were summarized using descriptive statistics
for the Pharmacodynamic population.

9

Reference ID: 5793951

A mixed-effects model for a crossover study was used to compare PD endpoints (e.g., Emax, Emin,
TA_AUE) between treatments. The model included treatment, period, sequence, and first-order carryover
effect as fixed effects, and subject nested within treatment sequence as random effect. If the carryover
effect was found to be nonsignificant at the 25% level, then the term was to be dropped from the model.
The residuals from the mixed-effects model were investigated for normality using the Shapiro-Wilk Wtest. If the probability value was ≥ 0.05, parameters were to be analyzed having a normal distribution. If
the probability value was < 0.05, parameters were to be analyzed using a nonparametric test such as the
Friedman’s test for the overall treatment effect. The Wilcoxon sign-rank test on the within-subject
differences was used for the pairwise treatment comparisons.
Reviewer’s comments: The Wilcoxon Sign Rank Test has an assumption that the distribution of paired
differences is symmetric. It should not be used on the distribution of differences between two treatments is
skewed. The analyses used by this reviewer for this study were consistent with those used in Study 450-EB
201-IR-00019 by the reviewer.
The treatment comparisons to assess the abuse potential of EB-1020 included the following:

•
•
•
•
•

d-AMP and placebo
LDX and placebo
each dose of EB-1020 and d-AMP
each dose of EB-1020 and LDX
each dose of EB-1020 and placebo

For study validity purposes, the primary endpoint, Emax of Drug Liking VAS, was compared
between the primary positive control (d-AMP) and placebo. If statistically significant, it would
confirm the sensitivity of the study.
For each of the contrasts or pairwise comparisons, the null hypothesis was that there was no treatment
effect difference between the tested pair, and the alternative hypothesis was that there was a treatment
effect difference between the tested pair.
All statistical tests were performed using two-tailed significance criteria using a 5% Type I error rate.
Reviewer’s comments: This study was completed in August 2014 before FDA guidance in this area was
published in 2017. All tests were proposed to be two sided with a significant level of 5%.
2.2.2. The sponsor’s pharmacodynamic results
•

Statistically significant differences were observed between placebo and both d-AMP (primary
control) and LDX on Drug Liking VAS Emax (primary endpoint), confirming study validity.
Both EB-1020 doses showed significantly higher Emax scores compared with placebo and were
not statistically different from d-AMP or LDX. However, Emin scores were significantly lower
for both EB-1020 compared with d-AMP, LDX and placebo, indicating significant disliking.

10

Reference ID: 5793951

•

•

•

•

•

Importantly, peak disliking occurred within 15 to 30 minutes of EB-1020 administration, whereas
mean peak liking was delayed, up to 2 to 3 hours following EB-1020 administration. Results for
Overall Drug Liking VAS were consistent with the results of Drug Liking VAS Emax; however,
Emax for Take Drug Again VAS indicated that subjects were less willing to take EB-1020 800
mg compared with d-AMP. Emax for Take Drug Again VAS indicated that subjects were less
willing to take EB-1020 800 mg compared with d-AMP.
Significant differences were observed between the active controls and placebo on all positive
effects endpoints (High VAS, Good Effects VAS). Emax and TA_AUE values for EB-1020 400
mg were generally not different or statistically lower than those derived for d-AMP and LDX. In
contrast, EB-1020 800 mg generally showed significantly higher scores on High VAS, but not
Good Effects VAS. Positive effects Emax and TA_AUE values for EB-1020 were significantly
higher than placebo.
Negative effects measures (Bad Effects VAS and Nausea VAS) revealed distinct aversive effects
of EB-1020. While d-AMP and LDX showed significant, but modest differences from placebo,
EB-1020 was associated with statistically greater negative effects Emax and TA_AUE values
compared with the active controls (800 mg only for TA_AUE) and placebo (800 mg > 400 mg).
Stimulant effects (ARCI-A, ARCI-BG, Alertness/Drowsiness VAS, Agitation/Relaxation VAS)
were most prominent with d-AMP and LDX in comparison to all other treatments. EB-1020 was
associated with significantly higher, but more modest Emax scores compared with placebo, and
had significantly lower scores on all ARCI endpoints compared with d-AMP and LDX, as well as
many of the VAS endpoints.
Any Effects VAS showed significant differences between the active controls and EB-1020
compared with placebo. EB-1020 800 mg had higher Emax and TA_AUE values compared with
d-AMP and LDX, whereas EB-1020 400 mg did not show a consistent pattern.
On Drug Similarity VAS, d -AMP and LDX were reported to be most similar to cocaine, damphetamine and ecstasy. In contrast, EB-1020 400 mg and 800 mg were not identified strongly
with any particular drug, although both doses were modestly identified as a stimulant (median
scores <25 on the 100-point scale).

2.2.3. Conclusion
Overall, the subjective effects profile for EB-1020 was unique compared with d-AMP and LDX. EB-1020
showed some similarities to active controls: liking, high and some stimulant-like effects. However, in
contrast to the active controls, EB-1020 was also associated with significant disliking and negative
effects, and was only rather weakly characterized as a stimulant, which could in part be related to its
prominent noradrenergic and serotonergic action rather than its dopaminergic activity. Physiological
measures also showed some similarities to d-AMP and LDX, as EB-1020 increased pulse rate and blood
pressure; however, the time course and magnitude were different from those of d-AMP and LDX and the
pattern of AEs was also different. The results of the present exploratory study indicate that EB-1020’s
subjective effects profile is distinct from that of d-AMP and LDX, and suggest a lower abuse potential
compared with these typical stimulants.

Reference ID: 5793951

2.3. Data Location

2.4. Reviewer’s Assessment
In this report, the reviewer used the following notations for treatments in Study EB-1020-IR-103:
d-AMP – d-AMP 40 mg
LDX – LDX 150 mg
P – Placebo
T400 – EB-1020 400 mg
T800 – EB-1020 800 mg
In the reviewer's analyses, inferential statistics were used for the primary endpoint (Drug Liking VAS
Emax) and key secondary endpoints (Overall Drug Liking VAS Emax and Take Drug Again VAS Emax).
For other endpoints, only descriptive statistics were presented. All tables and Figures were based on data
from 26 completers in the MCP.
2.4.1. Primary analysis
A total of 30 subjects completed the study, in which 4 subjects met the exclusion criteria for the MCP
(For Drug Liking VAS Emax, two completers did not respond to LDX, and two completers did not
respond to both dAMP and LDX), thus, 26 completers were included in the MCP.
2.4.1.1. Descriptive statistics
Table 1 summarizes the mean, standard deviation (SD), minimum (Min), first quartile (Q1), median
(Med), third quartile (Q3), and maximum (Max) for Drug Liking VAS Emax for each treatment, as well
as the paired differences in Drug Liking VAS Emax scores between treatments in the study.

12

Reference ID: 5793951

The peak mean responses for d-AMP and LDX were 68.0 and 71.2, at 2.0 and 2.5 hours post-dose,
respectively. For EB-1020, the peak mean responses were 64.2 at 2.5 hours (400 mg), and 65.6 at 3 hours
(800 mg) post-dose.
2.4.1.2. Statistical testing
Primary Endpoint: Drug Liking VAS Emax
For the primary endpoint, the study must demonstrate three key criteria:
1. Study Validation: At least one of the positive controls (d-AMP and LDX) must have
significantly larger mean responses than placebo, with a test value of 15, to validate the study.
(The sponsor used margin of 10 for this comparison).
2. Relative Abuse Potential: The mean difference between the positive control and the test drug
must be significantly greater than 0, demonstrating that all doses of EB-1020 have reduced drug
liking effects compared to both d-AMP and LDX.
3. Placebo Similarity: all doses of EB-1020 must demonstrate a mean difference from placebo that
is significantly less than 11, showing that drug liking for centanafadine is similar to placebo.

Statistical hypotheses
Test 1 (Study validation):

𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 ≤ 15 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 > 15

𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 ≤ 15 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 > 15

Test 2 (Relative abuse potential):

𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇400 ≤ 0 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇400 > 0

𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇800 ≤ 0 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑𝑑 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇800 >0

𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇400 ≤ 0 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇400 >0
𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇800 ≤ 0 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇800 >0

Test 3 (Placebo Similarity):

𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇400 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 ≥ 11 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇400 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 < 11
Testing procedure

𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇800 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 ≥ 11 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇800 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 < 11

This follows a sequential testing approach. An insignificant test result will terminate testing of subsequent
hypotheses. Therefore, no multiplicity adjustment for Type I error rate is required, and the significance
level for each test is 5%.

17

Reference ID: 5793951

For the mixed effects model including period, sequence, treatment, and carryover effect as fixed effects
and subject as a random effect, SAS Proc Mixed failed to find the optimal parameter estimates within the
default maximum number of iterations for Drug Liking VAS Emax. Consequently, the normality and
skewness of the paired treatment differences were evaluated. All paired differences either passed the W
test or had skewness values falling between -0.5 and 0.5 (inclusive); thus, the paired t-test was used for all
comparisons. Table 2 shows results from the primary analysis of Drug Liking VAS Emax.

The results from the primary analysis of Drug Liking VAS Emax show:
1. The mean differences between the positive controls (d-AMP and LDX) and placebo were
significantly greater than the test value of 15 (p <0.0001). Thus, the study is validated.
2. The mean differences between the positive controls (d-AMP and LDX) and each dose of EB1020 were not significantly greater than zero (p ≥ 0.0887), except for the comparison between
LDX and EB-1020 400 mg (p = 0.0106).
3. Due to the results from the comparison between the positive controls and each dose of EB1020, and based on the testing procedure, the comparison between each dose of EB-1020 and
placebo was not performed. However, the mean difference between each dose of EB-1020 and
placebo (>11, which exceeds the test value) clearly indicates that the test drug is not similar to
placebo.
2.4.2. Analyses on Overall Drug Liking VAS Emax and Take Drug Again VAS Emax
Overall Drug Liking VAS Emax and Take Drug Again VAS Emax are considered as two key secondary
endpoints in human abuse potential studies. These endpoints assess global drug effects based on subjects'
drug experience during the entire treatment period.

The hypotheses used for the key secondary endpoints are the following:
Statistical hypotheses

𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇400 = 0 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿𝐿 − 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇400 ≠ 0

Test 3:
𝐻𝐻0 : 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇400 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 = 0 𝑣𝑣𝑣𝑣. 𝐻𝐻𝑎𝑎 : 𝜇𝜇𝑇𝑇400 − 𝜇𝜇𝑃𝑃 ≠ 0

The significant level was 0.10 for the comparisons between EB-1020 and placebo. For other comparisons
the significant level was 0.05. The same statistical model used in the primary analysis was used for both
Overall Drug Liking VAS Emax and Take Drug Again VAS Emax.
For Overall Drug Liking VAS Emax, the normal assumption of the mixed effects model was not met (p =
0.0001). Consequently, the normality and skewness of the paired treatment differences were evaluated.
The paired t-test was used for the cases that p-value of the W test is greater than or equal to 0.05 or
skewness values fall between -0.5 and 0.5 (inclusive), and the Sign test otherwise. Table 5 shows results
from the analysis on Overall Drug Liking VAS Emax.

The results from the primary analysis of Overall Drug Liking VAS Emax show:

were significantly greater than the test value of 0 (p < 0.0001).

LDX and EB-1020 400 mg (p = 0.0004).

zero. However, due to the result in #2, and based on the testing procedure, these comparisons can
only be considered as exploratory.

between -0.5 and 0.5 (inclusive); thus, the paired t-test was used for all comparisons. Table 6 shows the
analysis results.

All descriptive statistics for the 7 secondary endpoints showed that EB-1020 produced larger mean and

32

Reference ID: 5793951

2.4.4. Summary Statistics for Drug Similarity VAS
The Drug Similarity VAS provides an estimate of drug class with which drug users identify the test drug.
The same VAS procedures used to assess subjective drug responses were used to assess perceived drug
similarity. Subjects were asked to compare the drug they received to a comparison drug on a scale ranging
from “Not at all similar” to “Very similar”. Each scale was scored as an integer from 0 to 100, where the
position of the subject’s response on the scale represented a defined integer. Drugs a subject has not
personally experienced often enough to use as a standard of comparison (based on the screening
assessment) were not included in his or her questionnaire.
Table 8 displays summary statistics for three choices of drugs (Cocaine, d-amphetamine, and ecstasy) that
produced larger means compared to other choice of drugs for each treatment for Drug Similarity VAS.

The number of subjects responding to each drug choice differed because the similarity question was only
asked of subjects who had prior experience with the drug. Study subjects were healthy recreational
stimulant users.
Based on the Drug Similarity VAS, subjects on average rated d-AMP as most similar to ecstasy; however,
only 7 subjects had prior experience with ecstasy. For LDX, mean similarity scores for both drug choices
(d-amphetamine and ecstasy) were comparable to each other and higher than those for d-AMP. Compared
to d-AMP and LDX, both doses of EB-1020 produced lower mean and median similarity scores for both
ecstasy and d-amphetamine.

Figure 30: The mean time course profiles for ARCI BG by treatment
The descriptive statistics based on ARCI A and ARCI BG showed that EB-1020 produced smaller
stimulant effects than d-AMP and LDX, with similar mean time course profiles between the two EB-1020
doses.

3. Conclusion
Based on 26 completers in the Agency-recommended modified completer population, the results from the
reviewer's primary analysis on Drug Liking VAS Emax and secondary analyses on the key secondary
endpoints (Overall Drug Liking VAS Emax and Take Drug Again VAS Emax) did not demonstrate that
EB-1020 has less abuse potential than d-AMP and LDX. However, with 26 subjects included in the
analyses, the power of statistical tests was low.
Descriptive statistics for the other seven VAS Emax secondary endpoints (Any Drug Effects, Bad Drug
Effects, Good Drug Effects, High, Alertness/Drowsiness, Agitation/Relaxation, and Nausea) showed that
compared to both d-AMP and LDX, EB-1020 produced similar (vs. LDX) to greater (vs. d-AMP)
euphoric effects at the 400 mg dose and higher euphoric effects at the 800 mg dose (vs. both d-AMP and
LDX). However, EB-1020 produced greater bad effects and nausea at both doses compared to the positive
controls. Analyses of Drug Similarity VAS and two ARCI Emax scores (A and BG) showed that EB1020 had less stimulant effect compared to d-AMP and LDX.
